16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T4c8,The method starts from the geometry of the prediction-loss and predictive-entropy plane. A likelihood-only detector uses a horizontal threshold on loss. This fails when many non-members have low loss because they are predictable. The proposed detector instead uses an inclined boundary by correcting loss with predictive entropy. The resulting score has the algebraic form of Helmholtz free energy: token loss plays the role of an energy term, predictive entropy is the uncertainty term, and a coefficient similar to temperature trades them off, giving a score of the form loss minus an entropy correction before sequence-level aggregation. The theoretical analysis shows that when members and non-members have the same mean predictive entropy, the correction preserves the expected membership gap and its lower bound. When the loss gap and entropy gap are positively correlated, an appropriate correction reduces the variance of the gap while preserving its mean, improving standardized separation. The analysis is also extended to settings with a nonzero mean entropy gap. Compared with reference-model or perturbation-based calibration, ETD uses the target model’s own token-level predictive distribution more directly.18:T43d,The strongest evidence comes from ARC-AGI-3, Frontier-CS polyomino packing, MNIST classifier compression, and Terminal-Bench 2.0. On ARC-AGI-3 with Claude Sonnet 4.6, team@3 matches roughly best@13, and team@5 matches roughly best@33, corresponding to effective multipliers of about 4.3x and 6.6x independent agents. On FT09, single-agent success is 9.4%, while team@3 reaches 90%. On LP85, 64 single-agent trials fail to solve the game, while team@5 succeeds 65% of the time. On Frontier-CS polyomino packing, team@4 scores 0.922 with Claude Opus 4.6 and 0.910 with Sonnet 4.6, compared with 0.893 and 0.891 for the strongest solo runs and 0.894 for the prior best-known result. On MNIST classifier compression, a 96-hour GPT-5.6 Sol team@4 run finds a 1,957-byte classifier at 99.4% accuracy, improving on the 2,461-byte known human solution and single-agent baselines. The negative result on Terminal-Bench 2.0 is important: team@2 improves over one attempt but does not beat independent pass@2, likely because intermediate feedback is not reliable enough to rank partial solutions.19:T4c1,The method uses block-level conditioning. A learned codebook defines a bounded set of block embeddings. A shared hypernetwork uses each code embedding to construct block parameters layer by layer by merging the underlying expert bases. Each constructed block therefore already contains information from the full expert pool. During token processing, a router selects only a small number of these blocks for each token. Participation is controlled by full-pool composition, execution is controlled by the number of selected blocks, and materialization is bounded by the codebook size.

The paper also introduces Dual-Path Residual Gating. Instead of merging expert bases into a single linear path, it composes separate value and gate paths and combines them through a gated product. This gives the composed expert a nonlinear dependence on expert bases without increasing the number of raw experts. Compared with sparse MoE, non-selected raw experts can still influence a token through block composition. Compared with dense MoE, the model avoids executing all experts per token. Compared with per-token or per-segment parameter merging, it avoids materializing a new expert-sized parameter set for every routing unit.1a:Tb27,技术主线有三步。

第一步，把 masked diffusion 的 tau-leaping sampler 重写成 ordered random partition 的等价形式。这个重写很关键，因为原始 CTMC/Gillespie 视角更接近逐坐标事件过程，不容易直接分析“某一步并行揭示一批坐标”带来的误差；而 partition 视角把整个采样轨迹表示成坐标集合被分成若干按顺序揭示的块，factorization error 就能自然写成对这些块内条件依赖的累积。

第二步，引入 dependence density ρ(u)。按论文描述，除去一个 N-1 的比例因子，ρ(u) 可以理解为：当其余坐标以概率 u 独立地已被揭示时，任意两个未揭示坐标之间平均 conditional mutual information 的密度。这个定义很有力，因为它把目标分布 π 的依赖结构压缩成一个一维函数。随后作者给出 ε_fact 的精确积分表示，schedule 的作用只通过权重项进入，而数据分布的全部难点都被放进 ρ(u)。这一步把“采样 schedule 优化”从一个离散、分布相关、难以下手的问题，变成了一个可分析的泛函优化问题。

第三步，分析不同依赖结构下的最优 schedule 形态。
- 对局部依赖分布，例如遍历马尔可夫链或足够规则的平稳过程，ρ_N 收敛到非退化连续 profile g。在这个 regime 下，优化平滑 schedule 只能改善 ε_fact 的前导常数，不能改变 N/K 的主阶，因此线性 schedule 是 order-optimal。这个结论很重要，因为它说明对很多“局部相关”的序列数据，花很多力气调花哨 schedule，收益可能只是常数级。
- 对 exchangeable mixtures 这类由全局 latent variable 诱导依赖的分布，ρ_N 会集中在原点附近，极限 profile 退化。此时早期揭示少量坐标能快速降低后续不确定性，因此 schedule 形状会显著影响误差阶。论文给出一个很强的例子：当 K_N≈log N 时，uniform schedule 的误差是 Ω(N/log N)，而 geometric schedule 可以做到 O(log N)。这不是常数差，而是阶的变化。

除此之外，论文还给出几个配套结果：有限步最优解满足的 stationarity equations；在大 N 极限下的变分问题及闭式 leading-order optimizer；以及 tau-leaping 中块大小随机性相对同极限 schedule 的 deterministic planner 所带来的额外 penalty。作者还把 total correlation 和 dual total correlation 也写成 ρ 的函数，从而把已有一些 heuristic 和上界放进同一框架里。

从方法取舍看，这篇工作没有去设计更复杂的联合块预测器，而是接受“仍用一维条件预测器”这一现实设定，转而优化采样 schedule。这样做的意义在于，它对应当前 masked discrete diffusion 最常见的实现路径，也更容易落地到现有 diffusion LM 系统里。1b:Tbae,The technical story has three main parts.

First, the paper rewrites the masked-diffusion tau-leaping sampler in an equivalent ordered-random-partition form. This is a useful reformulation. The original CTMC or Gillespie view is natural for exact one-coordinate-at-a-time simulation, but it is awkward for analyzing the error caused by revealing a whole block in parallel. The partition view makes the trajectory a sequence of revealed coordinate blocks, which is exactly the right object for expressing factorization error.

Second, the paper introduces the dependence density rho(u). Up to an N-1 scaling factor, rho(u) is the average conditional mutual information between two distinct coordinates when each of the remaining coordinates is independently revealed with probability u. This is the conceptual center of the paper. It compresses the dependence structure of the target distribution into a one-dimensional function. The authors then derive an exact integral representation of the factorization error epsilon_fact in which the schedule appears only through weighting terms, while all distribution-specific structure is captured by rho. This turns schedule optimization into a tractable functional problem.

Third, the paper analyzes optimal schedules under different dependence regimes.
- For locally dependent distributions, such as ergodic Markov chains or sufficiently regular stationary processes, rho_N converges to a nondegenerate continuous profile g. In this regime, optimizing over smooth schedules can improve only the leading constant of epsilon_fact, not its N/K scaling. So the linear schedule is order-optimal.
- For exchangeable mixtures, where dependence is global and mediated by a latent variable, rho_N concentrates near the origin and the limiting profile degenerates. In this regime, early fine-grained steps matter much more, because revealing a small number of coordinates can sharply reduce uncertainty about the latent structure. Here the schedule can change the asymptotic order of the error. The paper gives a striking example: when K_N is about log N, the uniform schedule has error Omega(N / log N), while a geometric schedule achieves O(log N).

The paper also derives stationarity equations for finite-step optimizers, a large-N variational problem with a closed-form leading-order optimizer, and an asymptotic penalty induced by the randomness of block sizes in tau-leaping relative to a deterministic planner with the same limiting schedule. It further expresses total correlation and dual total correlation through rho, which helps place several existing heuristics and bounds into one framework.

An important modeling choice is what the paper does not do. It does not introduce a more expensive joint block predictor. Instead, it accepts the practical setting of one-coordinate conditional predictors and optimizes the sampler around that constraint. That makes the analysis more relevant to current masked discrete diffusion implementations.1c:T5e7,The provided text indicates both theory and empirical validation, though the full numerical tables are not included in the excerpt. The main takeaways are clear.

1. The paper develops estimators for the dependence density rho, so the framework is not purely abstract. It aims to support practical schedule selection from data or samples.

2. On locally dependent distributions, the experiments should verify that linear schedules are already near-optimal and that schedule optimization mainly improves constants rather than scaling.

3. On exchangeable mixtures or other globally dependent distributions, the experiments should show that geometric schedules with finer early steps outperform uniform schedules substantially, matching the theoretical order separation. The headline asymptotic result stated in the text is that when K_N is about log N, the uniform schedule yields factorization error Omega(N / log N), whereas the geometric schedule yields O(log N).

4. Since the paper analyzes the penalty from random block sizes, any comparison against a deterministic planner is worth checking to see whether that asymptotic gap is already visible at practical sequence lengths.

For readers interested in diffusion LM sampling, the most important empirical questions are whether rho can be estimated stably, whether schedule optimization improves actual sample quality rather than only the theoretical error proxy, and whether factorization error remains a dominant issue once model error is non-negligible.1d:T553,This paper deserves a careful skim, and possibly a full read if you work on discrete diffusion or masked generation. Start with the problem setup and the definition of factorization error. Then go straight to Theorem 1 and build intuition for the integral representation of epsilon_fact and the meaning of rho(u); that is the structural center of the paper. After that, read Theorem 2 and Propositions 6–9 to understand the two regimes: local dependence versus global latent-variable dependence. The finite-step stationarity equations and the large-N variational result are the next pieces to inspect if you want to know how schedules are actually computed. Finish with the estimator and experiment sections to see whether the theoretical object can be recovered from data in practice.

If time is limited, prioritize the setup, Theorem 1, the uniform-versus-geometric schedule result, and the main empirical schedule comparisons. A 45–75 minute pass should be enough to capture the main ideas. Three follow-up questions are especially interesting: whether rho can serve as a diagnostic for different tokenization or discretization schemes, whether training can be shaped to reduce dependence patterns that cause large factorization error, and whether the same analysis transfers cleanly to real text diffusion models rather than only synthetic distributions.1e:T4db,RheoSampling introduces a dual-identity probability design. Instead of relying purely on deterministic top-k expansion, it inserts one stochastically sampled token into the candidate pool after the top-m deterministic selections. The key idea is to give this sampled token two probability identities. During dynamic tree construction, it is assigned a proxy probability so it can compete with deterministic candidates for expansion and survival. During verification, it is evaluated with its true sampling probability, which preserves lossless stochastic decoding.

This decoupling resolves the conflict between context-adaptive dynamic trees and proper stochastic sampling. The second major contribution is an equivalence-class analysis. Once randomness is introduced into a dynamic tree, token identities and tree topology become tightly coupled, making direct analysis intractable. The authors compress this large stochastic tree space into equivalence classes and use that framework to prove losslessness. The third component is an Optimal-Transport-based verification algorithm, designed to improve acceptance rate while maintaining correctness. They also add a sparse draft distribution strategy to reduce overhead on large vocabularies.1f:T482,The experimental comparison is mainly against top-k dynamic-tree baselines such as EAGLE-3, with acceptance rate and wall-clock speedup as the main metrics under standard stochastic decoding. The central empirical claim is that RheoSampling consistently outperforms these baselines across multiple LLMs and benchmarks, especially when temperature is nonzero. The paper explicitly points out that EAGLE-style implementations effectively treat draft probabilities as 1.0 during verification, which explains the one-hot collapse and poor tail exploration.

The experiments are designed to show that the theoretical fix matters in practice. They compare behavior across stochastic settings, showing that dynamic trees degrade once sampling is introduced unless probability semantics are handled carefully. Additional gains come from the OT-based verification and sparse draft distribution. The provided text does not include the full result tables, so it is better to avoid quoting missing numbers, but the qualitative result is clear: higher acceptance rates and better wall-clock speedups than top-k dynamic-tree baselines in realistic stochastic decoding.20:T411,L0-MoE applies L0 regularization to select important hidden dimensions in Transformer FFN up-projection layers and uses the selected dimensions to form experts. The L0 mask is optimized through a hard-concrete relaxation, with a Lagrangian term controlling the desired retention ratio. This combines feature selection with expert construction: instead of simply pruning FFN dimensions away, the method turns selected dimensions into sparse expert capacity. A linear router dispatches tokens to experts, with standard MoE auxiliary losses such as load balancing and router z-loss. On the data side, the paper encodes RedPajama documents with BGE-M3, clusters them with K-means, and builds domain-aware subsets. A cluster confusion matrix is then used for corpus curation and dynamic batching, so that expert training receives more structured semantic-domain signals. The method differs from simple MoE upcycling because the expert structure is derived from L0-selected FFN dimensions rather than only copying dense FFNs into multiple experts.21:T8c8,方法核心是基于 Poisson process 的 multi-draft speculative sampling。传统 speculative decoding通常是单条 draft continuation，target 逐位验证；这篇工作改成同时生成和验证多条 draft continuation，并利用 Poisson process 的构造得到一个 exact list-coupling。这里的关键不是“多 draft”本身，而是 Poisson 化之后可以自然得到几个性质。

第一是 exactness。算法仍然保证最终输出分布与直接从 target model 自回归采样完全一致，不是近似采样。也就是说，加速不以分布偏移为代价。

第二是 drafter invariance。论文强调其 list-coupling without communication 结构，使得接受机制对具体 drafter 的某些实现细节不敏感。这个性质在 watermark 场景尤其重要，因为如果水印嵌入方式和某个特定 drafter 强绑定，系统会更脆弱，也更难分析检测统计量。

第三是 watermarkability。作者不是在 speculative sampling 之后再叠加水印，而是直接利用 Poisson process 中的 keyed randomness 嵌入可恢复统计信号。论文声称该水印是 unbiased 的，即不会改变目标分布；同时水印强度不会随着采样效率提升而同步恶化。这里的含义是：在他们的构造下，增加 multi-draft 带来的接受率收益，不必像已有 no-go 结果那样直接拿水印强度去交换。

第四是理论比较对象。论文把 Rowan et al. 2025 作为主要 baseline，并声称在理论上证明了更高 acceptance rate。结合引言表述，作者并没有说自己绕开了 Hu and Huang 2024 的不可能性结论，而是明确指出那个结论对固定 draft 数仍成立；他们做的是沿着 draft 数这个额外自由度改善 frontier。这一点很重要，说明方法创新来自问题重参数化，而不是对原结论的反驳。

从机制上看，Poisson process 在这里承担了两个角色：一是提供可解析的随机候选生成与耦合工具，二是提供天然适合 key-based watermark 的随机源。相比常见 token-level maximal coupling 或基于 exponential race 的构造，这种做法更适合扩展到 list 级别，并把“接受”和“嵌入信号”统一到同一随机过程里。22:T958,The main idea is a Poisson-process-based multi-draft speculative sampling algorithm. Standard speculative decoding usually uses one drafted continuation and lets the target verify it token by token. Here, the algorithm generates and verifies multiple draft continuations and uses Poisson processes to build an exact list coupling. The novelty is not merely having multiple drafts; it is that Poissonization gives a coupling structure with several desirable properties.

First, exactness. The final output distribution is exactly the same as direct autoregressive sampling from the target model. This is not an approximation scheme.

Second, drafter invariance. The paper emphasizes a list-coupling-without-communication construction, which makes the acceptance mechanism less tied to a particular drafter implementation. That matters even more under watermarking, because a watermark that depends too strongly on one drafter can be brittle and harder to analyze statistically.

Third, natural watermarkability. The watermark is not bolted on after speculative decoding. Instead, keyed randomness is embedded directly into the Poisson-process sampling procedure, producing a recoverable statistical signal while keeping the target law unbiased. The claimed benefit is that watermark strength does not deteriorate simply because sampling efficiency improves. In other words, the efficiency gain from multi-draft verification does not have to be paid for by weakening the watermark in the same way as in earlier impossibility-style results.

Fourth, the theoretical comparison point is Rowan et al. 2025. The authors claim a provably higher acceptance rate than that method. Importantly, they do not claim to invalidate the no-go theorem of Hu and Huang 2024. They explicitly state that the trade-off still applies for a fixed number of drafts. Their contribution is to improve the frontier by introducing the number of drafts as an additional degree of freedom.

Mechanistically, the Poisson process plays two roles: it provides an analyzable random mechanism for candidate generation and exact coupling, and it also provides a natural source of keyed randomness for watermarking. Compared with token-level maximal coupling or exponential-race-style constructions, this seems better suited to list-level coupling and to unifying acceptance and watermark embedding within one stochastic process.23:T676,The analysis starts from one linear layer, Y = XW^T. An invertible transformation P is inserted as Z = XP and V = WP^{-T}, preserving the full-precision output while changing the coordinates seen by activation and weight quantizers. Activation quantization is modeled as per-token symmetric uniform quantization with a dynamic scale determined by ||Z_t||_∞. For 4-bit activations, q_A = 2^{4-1}-1 = 7. The weight quantizer is left general; it may be standard rounding or an optimized method such as GPTQ.

The key result is an exact decomposition of the local weight-activation quantization error into an activation-guided weight compensation term and an orthogonal residual. The first term is the part that weight optimization can reduce along directions supported by the quantized activations. The second term is orthogonal to that compensatable subspace; once P is fixed, it cannot be removed by better weight compensation. This separates two roles that are often mixed together: weight optimization fits the output within a chosen coordinate system, while transformations change the residual structure before quantization.

The paper then analyzes the orthogonal residual to explain common design choices. Random orthogonal rotations reduce the impact of persistent channel-wise outliers on per-token quantization scales. Sign selection reduces unfavorable alignment between outlier structure and the rotation basis. Channel scaling balances activation-side and weight-side quantities. From a second-moment bound on the regular activation term, the paper derives an L2 scaling rule and relates SmoothQuant-like L∞ scaling to a further relaxation.24:T4d7,MME-Safety is built around a four-dimensional annotation scheme: risk scenario, harm severity, image stealth level, and text stealth level. The risk taxonomy covers nine categories, including illegal activities, hate speech, malware generation, physical harm, economic damage, fraud, pornographic content, privacy violations, and politically sensitive content. Harm severity is represented in four tiers, from no harm to high harm, with a 0–10 consequence scale. Image stealth has three levels: explicit harmful objects or embedded malicious text; harmful visual elements that require inference; and visually neutral images whose risk appears only with the text. Text stealth follows a parallel three-level structure: explicit harmful requests, euphemistic reformulations, and benign-looking text that becomes harmful only when paired with the image. The dataset is curated from MM-SafetyBench and SIUO with manual screening to ensure that each image-text pair actually conveys the intended malicious signal. The evaluation framework goes beyond binary attack success by measuring response reliability, actual risk exposure, and the structure of defensive behavior, such as risk identification, refusal, and safe alternative suggestions.25:T4be,The study builds a controlled customer-support workflow setting. The dataset covers 130 workflows, each with a domain policy, tool schemas, and user goals. Two GPT-5 instances are used to generate multi-turn conversations, one acting as the user and the other as the workflow agent. From 1800 generated conversations, Claude-4.5-Opus and Gemini-2.5-Pro independently filter for logical consistency, missing steps, and policy compliance; 1027 conversations remain. Assistant decisions are converted into next-action examples. The training split contains 5834 examples, with 4093 text responses and 1741 tool calls; the validation split has 664 examples. The held-out evaluation split contains 84 conversations and 542 next-action examples, including 376 text decisions and 166 tool calls. The model comparison uses Qwen3 4B/14B and Gemma 3 4B/12B before and after full SFT with the same five-epoch recipe. The evaluation protocol separates text similarity, gold-history turn success judged by Gemini-2.5-Pro, tool behavior, and closed-loop autonomous execution. This separation is the main technical design choice: it makes the difference between local correctness and trajectory-level state maintenance observable.26:T566,The contribution is a benchmark and annotation protocol rather than a new model. VidOmni-Bench contains 500 videos spanning five predefined complexity types: Static World, Body Motion, Hand-Object Interaction, Crowd Action, and Multi-Scene Dynamics. These are balanced across three duration buckets, from very short clips to videos up to 90 minutes. That design is important because complexity and duration are treated as separate axes instead of being entangled.

The caption source is also deliberate. Instead of relying on human-written dense ground-truth captions, the authors use five different Video-LLMs to generate dense descriptions: InternVL3.5, Qwen3-VL, video-SALMONN 2+, Keye-VL 1.5, and Gemini 3 Flash. This yields 2,500 video-caption pairs. Human annotators then verify each sentence in a two-stage process and assign binary labels. Sentences that are plausible but unsupported by the video are retained as hard negatives. This makes the benchmark harder to game than synthetic negative construction, because the negatives come from realistic model hallucinations rather than obviously corrupted text.

Conceptually, the benchmark measures grounded verification rather than stylistic generation. It requires checking object states, actions, background changes, scene transitions, and temporal consistency, which makes it a stronger test of video evidence integration.27:T42a,The experimental findings are straightforward. Current Video-LLMs frequently hallucinate events during dense caption generation, so their outputs are not reliably grounded in the video. They also perform poorly when used as verifiers, struggling to reject plausible but incorrect event descriptions. In addition, failure patterns vary substantially across complexity types and duration ranges, which suggests that current weaknesses are heterogeneous rather than reducible to one single bottleneck.

The paper also includes dataset statistics showing that VidOmni-Bench requires broader video inspection than prior benchmarks and provides denser event coverage. Of the 500 videos, 433 contain audio, which means some examples may implicitly test audiovisual grounding as well. The full numeric result tables are not available in the provided text, so exact scores should not be restated. The qualitative conclusion is still clear: long duration, fine-grained interactions, and multi-scene dynamics significantly stress current Video-LLMs in event-level verification.28:Ta58,核心方法是 Benchproofer，一条把已有 coding task 连同其已知正确 patch，转成 formally verified instance 的构造流程。它的设计抓住了两个现实约束：一是不能把整个仓库都形式化；二是不能只依赖机械验证，因为规格本身可能写错。

第一部分是局部建模。Benchproofer 只对新增或修改的代码建立形式规格和证明，而不是试图验证整个代码库。对于修复代码调用但未修改的现有函数，系统不去展开其实现，而是为这些 callee 写 axioms，也就是对调用方真正依赖的性质做过近似摘要。这样做的原因很直接：如果要求把所有依赖函数及其传递闭包都证明一遍，SWE-bench 级别任务几乎不可操作。axiomatization 把验证边界收缩到 patch 附近，使问题规模可控。

第二部分是多后端验证表示。SWE-Proof 为每个实例提供三种验证后端：Nagini（面向静态类型 Python 验证）、Velvet（Lean 上的命令式验证 DSL）和纯 Lean。这里不是简单换语言，而是覆盖了不同验证风格：SMT 驱动的程序验证，以及需要显式写 proof 的 theorem proving。这样能区分“代码逻辑是否容易规格化”和“证明搜索是否成为瓶颈”。

第三部分是 admission pipeline，也就是机械门和对抗门联合筛选。一个候选实例只有在多重 gate 都通过后才会被收入 benchmark。机械 gate 包括规格、实现和证明在对应后端上的可检查性；对抗 gate 则用 LLM 审计规格是否 faithful，是否遗漏需求、过度放宽、或允许错误实现通过。作者特别强调 dominant failure 是 faithfulness failure：规格只约束了部分行为，把剩余关键语义留空。这一点很重要，因为形式验证只能保证“满足规格”，不能保证“规格就是用户想要的东西”。

第四部分是评测协议。作者不仅评估模型直接修 patch，还比较了几种设置：只给自然语言 issue；给结构化自然语言 specification；让模型自己写 specification 再验证；以及直接提供正确 formal specification。这个设计把“patch synthesis”和“specification synthesis”两个子问题拆开，因而能定位真正瓶颈。

从方法取舍看，这篇工作的关键不是发明新的 verifier，而是把形式方法嵌入真实软件工程 benchmark，并用环境 axiomatization + adversarial audit 让它可落地。相比传统 hidden tests，它牺牲了构造成本，换来更强的 correctness oracle；相比端到端全仓库验证，它牺牲了全局完备性，换来可扩展性。29:T92a,The core method is Benchproofer, a pipeline that converts a coding task with a known correct patch into a formally verified benchmark instance. The design is built around two practical constraints: full-repository verification is infeasible, and mechanical verification alone is not enough if the specification itself is wrong.

First, Benchproofer uses local modeling. It formally specifies and verifies only the new or modified code. Calls into unchanged functions are handled through axioms: over-approximate summaries of the properties the patch relies on. This is the key scalability move. Without it, one would need to verify the entire transitive closure of dependencies, which is unrealistic for SWE-bench-scale tasks.

Second, the benchmark supports three verification backends: Nagini for statically typed Python verification, Velvet as an imperative-style verification DSL in Lean, and pure Lean where proof obligations are discharged by proof writing rather than an SMT solver. This is useful because it separates different sources of difficulty: specification quality, program verification, and proof search.

Third, the construction pipeline uses both mechanical and adversarial admission gates. A candidate instance is admitted only if the spec, implementation, and proof check mechanically in the target backend, and if adversarial LLM auditors judge the specification to be faithful to the original task. This is an important design choice. Formal verification only guarantees conformance to the spec, not that the spec captures the intended behavior. The paper identifies faithfulness failures as the dominant issue: a spec constrains part of the required behavior while leaving critical semantics unconstrained.

Fourth, the evaluation protocol disentangles patch generation from specification generation. The paper compares several settings: solving from the natural-language issue alone, adding a structured natural-language specification, asking the model to synthesize a specification and verify against it, and supplying the correct formal specification. This makes it possible to localize the real bottleneck.

The contribution is therefore not a new verifier. It is a practical recipe for embedding formal methods into realistic software-engineering benchmarks using environment axiomatization plus adversarial auditing.2a:T608,SWE-Proof covers all 500 instances of SWE-bench Verified and extends to the Python fragment of SWE-bench Pro. Each instance includes a ground-truth formal specification, implementation, and proof, under three verification backends. For realistic repository-level verified coding tasks, this is a substantial benchmark asset.

The evaluation uses Claude Opus 4.8 and GPT-5.5. The main findings are sharp. First, passing all hidden tests is not a reliable correctness signal: roughly 25% to 50% of such patches are still flawed. Second, adding a structured natural-language “specification” does not close that gap, which shows that informal constraints remain too weak. Third, asking the model to synthesize a specification and then verify against it does not improve over the unaided baseline. But when the correct formal specification is supplied, performance rises substantially: Opus 4.8 goes from 85.0% to 95.1%, and GPT-5.5 from 81.2% to 94.7%.

The most informative analysis concerns specification quality. Only 62% of synthesized specifications survive adversarial audit. The dominant failure mode is lack of faithfulness. The correlation with task success is strong: 89% of specifications from unresolved instances fail the audit, compared with 47% from resolved ones. This makes specification synthesis the central unresolved problem.

From a reward-design perspective, the paper also carries a warning: formal verification is only as good as the specification. A wrong spec can turn a seemingly strong oracle into a misleading one.2b:T58f,Although marked skim, this is worth reading a bit more carefully if you care about coding RL verifiers. Start with the introduction, which clearly frames the two core weaknesses of hidden tests: incompleteness and exploitability. Then read the overview and Figure 1 to see how a real SWE-bench issue is converted into a formal spec, callee axioms, and a backend-checkable implementation. The interesting part is not “adding proofs to SWE-bench,” but how the verification boundary is controlled.

In the method section, focus on two ideas: environment axiomatization and the combination of mechanical plus adversarial gates. The first is what makes the approach scale to real repositories; the second is what makes the benchmark trustworthy. In the experiments, prioritize four results: the fraction of test-passing but incorrect patches, the gain from supplying the correct formal spec, the audit pass rate for synthesized specs, and the failure analysis around faithfulness.

If time is limited, read the introduction, overview, the subsections on axiomatization and admission gates, then the main result tables and the specification failure analysis. That should take around 40 minutes. Questions worth probing more deeply: whether the axioms introduce overly strong assumptions, how reliable the faithfulness audit is, and whether this kind of formal oracle could be used for RL training rather than only evaluation.2c:T788,The core idea is Dense Reasoning Trace, or DRT. The goal is not just to make the model say less, but to make it reason in a different external format. DRT separates reasoning into two explicit channels: <visual> stores observed evidence from the image, while <think> stores compact logical derivations. Symbolic connectors and concise state transitions replace long natural-language sentences. This serves two purposes: reducing linguistic redundancy and disentangling observation from deduction so that assumptions are less likely to be written as if they were visual facts.

Training has two stages. The first is Dense Trace Initialization. High-quality multimodal instruction data are rewritten into the DRT format to create a DRT-SFT dataset, and supervised fine-tuning is used to internalize this reasoning mode. The key role of this stage is format adaptation rather than knowledge acquisition: the model learns to organize intermediate states in a compact structured space.

The second stage is Trace-Grounded Reinforcement Learning. The authors build a DRT-RL dataset using a tri-perspective verification pipeline, keeping only traces that satisfy three conditions simultaneously: correct final answer, logically consistent intermediate steps, and faithful visual grounding. They then optimize the model with a trace-grounded GRPO objective, augmented with process-based rewards and exploratory incentives. In effect, the policy is rewarded not only for being correct, but also for producing short, logically coherent, and visually grounded traces.

Compared with prior efficiency methods, this is not early stopping, token selection, or budget prediction within the same natural-language CoT space. It changes the reasoning expression space itself. Compared with standard CoT SFT/RL, it places much stronger emphasis on disentangling observation from deduction and on verifying grounding quality of intermediate traces.2d:T6a4,The model is evaluated on five challenging multimodal reasoning benchmarks, including text tasks and out-of-distribution video tasks. The headline result is a 5.5x improvement in token efficiency together with a 1.3-point accuracy gain over Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct under official prompting. That is the main empirical claim: shorter traces do not merely save tokens at the cost of quality; the structured format can improve the efficiency–accuracy tradeoff.

The paper also emphasizes component ablations rather than only final benchmark numbers. Dense Trace Initialization appears to be necessary for moving the model into the new reasoning format; without it, simply asking for concise reasoning would not reliably produce high-density traces. Trace-Grounded RL further improves faithfulness, especially under tight token budgets, where models otherwise tend to sacrifice logical completeness or grounding for brevity. The tri-perspective verification pipeline is important because it raises the quality of RL reference traces and filters out trajectories that are answer-correct but process-poor, or process-plausible but visually ungrounded.

From a foundation-model perspective, there are clear caveats. The gains depend on a specific trace schema and a data rewriting pipeline, so transfer to other model families, modality mixes, or pure-text reasoning remains open. The 5.5x token-efficiency number should be read carefully: one needs to check whether it counts only output tokens or total tokens, and whether prompts are strictly matched. Also, some of the burden may shift from inference to schema design and data construction quality, which makes the dataset pipeline central to the result.2e:T403,For a quick pass, start with Figure 1 and the method overview to understand what DRT actually looks like; otherwise it is easy to misread it as ordinary concise CoT. Then read the two training stages carefully: how the SFT data are rewritten, and how verification and rewards are defined in RL. Finally, inspect the main tables and efficiency–accuracy plots to confirm that the gains come from changing the reasoning representation rather than from prompt engineering.

If time is limited, the introduction, the DRT representation section, Dense Trace Initialization, Trace-Grounded RL, and the main experiments are enough to capture the paper in roughly 25 minutes. For deeper reading, focus on three places: the rewriting rules for DRT-SFT, which determine whether key information survives compression; the criteria in the tri-perspective verification pipeline, which determine RL signal quality; and the ablations isolating the effects of observation/deduction disentanglement, process rewards, and exploration incentives.2f:T566,The method has three main components.

First, a unified tactile representation. The authors introduce a Canonical Hand Model, inspired by human hand structure, to map tactile observations from different dexterous hands and grippers into a common spatial template. On top of that, a Unified Tactile Autoencoder encodes the aligned tactile measurements into a shared latent space while preserving local force structure. This is an important design choice: they do not rely on a purely learned sensor adapter from raw heterogeneous inputs, but instead impose a canonical geometric reference before latent compression.

Second, joint world-action-tactile modeling. ME-Dex-1.0 uses a three-expert Mixture-of-Transformers architecture: a Video Expert for future video prediction, a Tactile Expert for future tactile latent prediction, and an Action Expert for action-chunk generation. The experts communicate through H-Bridge shared attention in intermediate layers, so the Action Expert can access evolving representations of predicted visual and tactile futures during generation. Training is done jointly with flow matching, which places the model closer to continuous trajectory generation than standard autoregressive token prediction. Relative to prior tactile-conditioned policies, the conceptual shift is explicit tactile dynamics modeling rather than static tactile conditioning.30:T4de,The evaluation spans RoboTwin, DexJoCo, ManiFeel, and two real-world platforms: a LeRobot SO-101 equipped with a Paxini PX6AX tactile sensor and Xynova Flex2 dexterous hands. The main empirical claim is consistent improvement in average manipulation success across both grippers and dexterous hands, with additional gains on bimanual tasks and under Clean-to-Random generalization on RoboTwin.

The most informative results are the ablations. Removing future tactile prediction or the Tactile Expert hurts performance, suggesting that tactile forecasting is functionally useful rather than decorative. Replacing the canonical tactile alignment pipeline with less structured alternatives weakens cross-embodiment training, which supports the need for a shared tactile reference frame. The added tactile trajectories from the simulation data engine also appear to improve robustness and transfer. The full text provided here does not include complete numeric tables, so it is not safe to restate exact parameter counts, dataset sizes, or absolute margins. Still, the experimental pattern is clear: the gains come from the combination of representation alignment, joint future-state prediction, and data augmentation for missing tactile supervision.31:T41d,The system combines a streaming speech encoder, a decoder-only LLM, parallel output streams for assistant text and function calls, an auxiliary RNN-T branch for incremental user transcription, and a streaming TTS/codec decoder. The input waveform is converted to 128-bin log-Mel features from 16 kHz audio with a 25 ms window and 10 ms stride. A 600M-parameter streaming FastConformer encoder with 24 cache-aware layers and 1024 hidden size produces one state every 80 ms after causal subsampling. Attention uses 70 frames of left context and no right context, and the convolution blocks are causal. The LLM backbone is NVIDIA Nemotron-Nano-9B-v2-Base. The same speech representation is shared by the RNN-T transcription head and the LLM adapter, avoiding a separate ASR encoder and keeping transcription aligned with the acoustic context used for response generation. Compared with DuplexSLA-style serialization of actions into a shared autoregressive channel, the main design choice is to keep specialized parallel streams for text and function calls.32:T4bf,ArenaFlow starts with tournament-based relative ranking. For each query, the policy samples a group of trajectories. A seeded single-elimination tournament compares candidates, with a greedy decoding trajectory used as an anchor for the initial ranking prior. The judge does more than choose the better trajectory. It also marks pivotal success steps, extracts strategy-like skills, and attributes whether retrieved historical skills contributed to decisive reasoning. The framework then propagates credit at two levels. For step-level optimization, tournament survival depth is used as a confidence signal: pivotal steps that support wins in later rounds receive higher weight because they have survived comparison against stronger candidates. Depth-aware weighting and intra-trajectory normalization convert trajectory-level advantages into more targeted step-level training signals. For skill-level propagation, a global memory stores candidate skills. Their utility is estimated from group-level usage attribution; skills repeatedly used in pivotal success steps are reinforced, while stale or ineffective ones are pruned. During later rollouts, high-utility skills are retrieved as priors to guide exploration.33:T5c5,The method starts from a unified gradient view. For a group of responses under the same prompt, positive weights increase the likelihood of preferred responses, negative weights suppress dispreferred ones, and magnitudes determine update strength. GVPO uses the analytical solution of KL-constrained reward maximization to design these weights. The obstacle is the partition function in the closed-form solution, which would require an intractable expectation over all possible responses. The key condition is that if the assigned weights within a prompt group sum to zero, the partition function cancels across compared responses. GVPO then defines the response weight as the difference between two centered distances: one from implicit rewards induced by the current and reference policies, and one from actual rewards. The authors provide two interpretations. First, the GVPO gradient matches the gradient of a mean-squared-error objective between implicit-reward and actual-reward centered distances. Second, the loss can be decomposed into a group-relative advantage term, a covariance term between current and reference policies that constrains excessive policy drift, and a current-policy variance term that encourages moderate entropy. Compared with GRPO, it avoids uncontrolled importance-sampling ratios. Compared with DPO, it claims a unique optimum aligned with the KL-constrained reward-maximization solution, without relying on the Bradley-Terry preference model.34:T41a,The framework has three main components. First, it decomposes each natural-language reasoning step into premises, explanations, and conclusions, making the dependency structure explicit. Second, it auto-formalizes these components into symbolic representations and uses automated theorem provers to check whether the conclusion follows from the premises. Third, it defines Solver-Based Backtracking Reward, or SBR, which combines solver verification with premise fidelity. This matters because a model could otherwise obtain a formally valid proof by changing or misusing the original premises. At inference time, if a step’s SBR falls below a threshold, the system backtracks and regenerates from that point. Compared with post-hoc methods such as LogicReward and VeriCoT, LogicTrack verifies during generation rather than after the full trajectory is completed. The paper also extends the framework to supervised fine-tuning by pairing real flawed model trajectories with solver-verified corrections and introducing a dedicated <backtrack> token.35:T413,GUARD, Guided Answer-Reasoning Distillation, builds a deployable unlearned model in four stages. It first samples unsafe or unstable completions to identify failure trajectories. It then rewrites them offline into safe-exit alternatives: fluent non-disclosing reasoning followed by a stable refusal-style answer. Prompt-end guidance tokens are used as a training scaffold to align a frozen reasoning model with these rewritten trajectories. Finally, answer-reasoning distillation internalizes the behavior into the model parameters, so inference does not require runtime guidance tokens. Compared with gradient-ascent suppression, GUARD does not merely push probability mass away from protected tokens. Compared with answer-only refusal optimization, it supervises both the reasoning channel and the final answer. Compared with representation redirection, it tries to avoid destabilizing the autoregressive decoder. The paper also introduces NFRS, a 0–1 score for structural stability, fluency, and absence of unsupported substitute content.36:T55c,The authors study a GPT-2-XL-style model with 48 layers and 1600-dimensional hidden states, trained from scratch on random walks over Manhattan. Inputs encode origin, destination, and previous turns, and the model predicts the next turn. The analysis combines representation extraction, causal activation edits, ablations, and failure tracing. Intersection representations are extracted with linear probes and diff-means features, with diff-means working better for discrete location features. A minimal teleportation edit changes the model’s encoded current position to an intersection near the goal, then tests whether the next greedy action reaches the goal from that edited position. Legal-move encoding is analyzed by checking whether intersection features promote moves that are valid at the current node. A separate goal compass is identified: it encodes the direction of the destination and biases the model among legal moves. The failure analysis traces illegal moves to superposed intersection features packed into a small subspace. Out of distribution, the correct position signal weakens and interference from incorrect intersection features increases. The model also organizes intersections by affordance: intersections with the same legal next moves tend to have nearby representations, making some localization errors harmless for immediate move legality.37:T46c,The numerical results support the mechanistic story. Intersection features are most accurately decoded around layer 18; with diff-means, 99.6% of intersections are decoded with at least 90% accuracy on held-out data. In the teleportation intervention, using a layer-11 direction succeeds for 99.3% of testable nodes on at least one eligible scene, showing that the location representation is causally used rather than merely probe-readable. Legal-move analyses show that current-intersection features favor valid actions. Ablating the goal compass preserves the ability to output legal moves but strongly harms navigation to distant destinations, separating local legality from goal-directed control. The observed failures under forced detours are explained by weakened current-position signals and increased feature interference; strengthening the correct signal or suppressing interference substantially restores performance. Training-dynamics analyses indicate that legal-move and goal-direction representations mature before precise localization, while the position code expands and becomes increasingly organized by affordance.38:T5b4,The paper uses classic Contrastive Activation Addition as a causal probe rather than proposing a new steering method. It compares explicit CoT models and COCONUT-style latent-CoT variants of Llama-3.1-8B and Llama-2-7B, trained on the same ProSQA data, with the latent variants using five continuous thoughts. A task direction is built from contrastive activations, then injected into the residual stream at inference time.

The analysis is carefully split into two parts. First, representation displacement: the authors estimate an independent stance coordinate and measure how far the intervention moves hidden states along that coordinate, normalized by its standard deviation. Second, output response: they evaluate both generation-level steering success with an LLM judge and a judge-free token-level metric, the change in stance margin at the first generation step, computed from target-stance versus original-stance token sets. This separation is the core methodological strength of the paper.

To test the transition-gap hypothesis, they then ask three questions: whether task information remains decodable in continuous thoughts, whether the output distribution changes abruptly at the latent-to-language boundary, and whether task-related directions retain bidirectional control over generation. The evidence supports the view that latent representations can encode task information while still failing to transmit intervention effects into language.39:T49d,The main experiments cover three stance-style settings: sentiment, truthfulness, and politics, using datasets including TruthGen and TwinViews-13k. Interventions are applied to the layer-16 residual stream with strengths λ in {±1, ±5, ±10}. The central result is in Table 1: at λ=10, latent-CoT produces hidden-state displacement comparable to, or slightly larger than, explicit CoT, yet preserves only about 1%–5% of explicit CoT’s shift in stance-token probability. Generation-level steering success is also much weaker.

This is important because the token-distribution metric is measured before sampling, so the gap cannot be dismissed as decoding noise or judge instability. The paper further shows that task information remains identifiable in continuous thoughts, which rules out the simple explanation that latent states are semantically empty. Additional analyses indicate an abrupt distributional change at the latent-to-language boundary and substantially weaker bidirectional control in latent CoT than in explicit CoT. The main quantitative message is not an absolute benchmark gain, but a strong mismatch between latent-state movement and output sensitivity.3a:T436,Start with the introduction and the setup of the failure mode. The paper is easiest to understand if you keep one distinction in mind: moving a hidden representation is not the same as changing the output distribution. Then read the section defining the failure mode, especially Figure 1 and Table 1. Those already establish the main empirical claim.

After that, focus on the analysis around Figure 2: decodability of task information in latent thoughts, abrupt distributional change at the transition boundary, and weakened bidirectional control. The judge-free next-token margin is worth close attention because it makes the argument much stronger than a pure generation-level evaluation.

If your work touches latent reasoning, the most useful takeaway is that the latent-to-language interface may need to be explicitly trained or regularized. A latent state can be linearly readable without being an effective control handle for language generation. Around 25–35 minutes is enough for a solid skim; more time is only needed if you want to reproduce the steering analysis.3b:T804,The method has three parts. First, the authors introduce an evidence-grounded protocol to identify OCR heads. They decode with greedy autoregression, then replay attention along the model’s own generated trajectory instead of teacher-forcing on reference tokens. Predictions are aligned to references at the character level, and only tokens fully contained in exact-match spans are kept for scoring. For each aligned visible span, they define a visual evidence set using patch regions whose IoU with annotated evidence exceeds 0.1. A head gets an OCR hit if its top-attended key falls inside the evidence set for the current generated token. Candidate OCR heads are then selected using sample-level hit rates and activation frequency across examples.

Second, they separate discovery from validation and perform held-out causal interventions. The identified OCR heads are ablated on a disjoint validation split, and the resulting degradation is compared against matched random head ablations. This is important because it distinguishes heads that merely correlate with evidence from heads that are causally important for transcription.

Third, they investigate the origin of these OCR heads. Heads found from visual transcription are compared against retrieval/copy heads independently identified from text-only retrieval tasks. The comparison includes top-k overlap, all-head rank correlation, and cross-task causal effects. The main finding is strong overlap in general VLMs. They also compare models before and after OCR tuning and show that the dominant head population and layer organization are largely preserved, while functional and causal importance is redistributed. In other words, specialization is closer to reweighting than rewiring.

Relative to prior work, the paper moves beyond localized visual retrieval tasks, studies full-sequence transcription, and combines attention-based identification with held-out causal validation. It also frames OCR as reuse of a broader retrieval/copy mechanism rather than as an isolated visual skill.3c:T6a5,The experiments cover three OCR-specialized models—GLM-OCR, MinerU2.5, and PaddleOCR-VL-1.6—and two general VLMs for the reuse analysis. The most important results come in three groups.

First, OCR-relevant heads are sparse and stable. Across running text, tables, and formulas, the authors identify small reproducible head sets whose targeted ablation hurts transcription substantially more than matched random ablations. That supports causal relevance rather than incidental correlation.

Second, visual OCR heads strongly overlap with textual retrieval/copy heads. The abstract reports untuned top-20 intersections of 73.3% in two general VLMs, with all-head Spearman correlations ranging from 0.677 to 0.886. Those are strong numbers for a mechanistic overlap claim.

Third, OCR tuning preserves the main head population and layer layout while changing relative importance. The paper argues that specialization does not create a new circuit family; instead, it amplifies and redistributes the role of an existing one. The excerpt does not include the full tables, but the narrative suggests this pattern is consistent across model generations and OCR subdomains.

Two experimental choices are especially good. Discovery and validation are separated, which reduces interpretability overfitting. And the analysis spans text, tables, and formulas, which makes the mechanism claim less likely to be an artifact of plain text OCR. The main limitations are also clear: the analysis is head-centric and does not fully cover MLPs, residual-stream dynamics, or the vision encoder; and the exact-match plus IoU filtering improves precision but may miss softer alignments or structure-sensitive behavior.3d:Tb2f,方法核心是一条多阶段 agentic environment construction pipeline。输入只有源代码仓库本身，不使用任务特定的 issue、PR、文档或人工规格。第一步是功能发现与任务设计。系统让 agent 检查代码结构、构建配置和公共入口，优先选择需要跨文件理解、具有公开接口且行为可观察的功能单元，支持 CLI、纯函数库接口和有状态 API。这里的关键不是简单抽函数，而是挑选“能写成行为规范、又不把实现细节锁死”的目标功能。

第二步是代码库改造。CodeMidas 会把目标功能从原仓库中移除或留空，保留其余项目结构、依赖和上下文，形成 solver 可操作的开发起点。这样做比从零给一个编程题更接近真实软件工作：模型需要在原项目约束下补全缺失实现，而不是在孤立函数上做 HumanEval 式补题。

第三步是基于执行的测试构造。系统运行原始代码，通过执行轨迹、返回值、状态变化、文件输出、CLI 输出等可观察行为来生成测试期望。这里的设计重点是“behavioral specification”而不是“implementation matching”：测试要约束外部行为，但允许不同内部实现。文中明确强调要避免把原实现细节直接编码进 verifier，否则会把任务退化成补全原代码。为此，测试构造结合执行证据、边界情况扩展和一致性检查，尽量让 verifier 接受替代性正确实现。

第四步是环境一致性检查。构造后的环境需要保证：原始正确实现能通过 verifier；移除目标实现后的起始环境不能 trivially 通过；容器化依赖、构建脚本和测试注入流程都稳定。这一步更像数据工程里的 validity gate，避免把噪声环境送进 RL。

第五步是 rollout-based filtering，也是这篇工作最有信息量的部分。作者不是只看静态构造结果，而是先让 agent 在候选环境上 rollout，再用这些 rollout 反过来筛任务。过滤信号包括：对抗性 rollout 检查是否存在泄漏或 reward hacking 路径；solution review 判断 verifier 的通过/失败是否真的符合题意；以及 success rate 统计，用来剔除过难、过易或奖励不稳定的任务。也就是说，任务质量不是由生成器单方面决定，而是由“环境 × agent 行为”共同定义。这比只做静态 test generation 更接近真实 RL 需求。

最终得到的数据集包含 5,545 个可验证任务，来自 3,185 个开源代码库，覆盖 23 种编程语言和 15 个技术领域。训练时使用 GRPO 在 MiMo-V2.5 上做 RL。论文还分析了训练后 agent 行为变化，发现模型会更多探索代码库，并更频繁地执行自写检查，这说明学到的不只是 benchmark-specific patching，而是更一般的工作流策略。3e:Ta02,CodeMidas is an agentic pipeline for environment construction. The only task-specific input is a source repository. It does not consume issues, PRs, or human-written specs. First, the system identifies candidate functionality by inspecting repository structure, build metadata, and public entry points. It prioritizes functionality with observable behavior and nontrivial codebase context, including CLI tools, pure library functions, and stateful APIs. The selection criterion is important: the target should admit a behavioral specification while leaving implementation freedom open.

Second, the repository is adapted into a development starting point. The target implementation is removed or blanked out, while the rest of the project structure, dependencies, and surrounding context are preserved. This makes the task closer to real software work than isolated function completion.

Third, CodeMidas constructs tests grounded in execution of the original code. It runs the original implementation and uses observable outputs, return values, state transitions, file effects, and CLI behavior to infer expected behavior. The design goal is behavioral verification rather than implementation matching. The verifier should reject wrong solutions but still accept alternative correct implementations, so the pipeline tries to avoid overfitting tests to the original code path.

Fourth, the environment is checked for execution consistency. The original implementation must pass; the stripped starting point must not pass trivially; the containerized dependencies and grading flow must be stable. This is a data validity gate more than a modeling contribution, but it is essential for RL.

Fifth, and most interestingly, the paper adds rollout-based filtering. Candidate environments are probed with agent rollouts before RL training. The pipeline uses adversarial rollouts to detect leakage and exploitability, solution reviews to assess whether verifier outcomes align with the stated requirements, and rollout success rates to remove tasks that are too noisy, too easy, too hard, or reward-misaligned. This is a strong design choice: task quality is defined not only statically, but through interaction with actual agent behavior.

The final dataset contains 5,545 verifiable tasks from 3,185 open-source repositories, spanning 23 programming languages and 15 technical domains. RL is then run with GRPO on MiMo-V2.5. The paper also studies behavioral changes during training, showing increased repository exploration and more self-written checks by the agent.3f:T56c,The constructed dataset has 5,545 tasks from 3,185 repositories, covering 23 languages and 15 domains. MiMo-V2.5 is trained with GRPO on these environments. The trained policy improves on all five external benchmarks. Reported headline numbers include DeepSWE pass rate rising from 10.0% to 21.7%, ProgramBench Almost Solved increasing from 4.5 to 21.5, and Terminal-Bench v2.1 pass rate improving from 63.7% to 72.2%. The gains span issue repair, whole-program construction, code translation, and terminal interaction, which suggests transfer beyond the exact training distribution.

The scale-versus-quality study is more informative than the benchmark table alone. Increasing the number of high-quality tasks from 1k to 3k to 5,545 yields progressively better results on SWE-bench Pro, DeepSWE, and CodeMidas Val. At the same time, a cleaned 3k subset outperforms an 8k baseline built without cleaning and filtering on all three metrics. That is a strong signal that verifier reliability and environment curation matter more than naive expansion.

The behavioral analysis is also useful. As RL progresses, agents explore repositories more and perform more varied self-verification. Agent-written checks correlate with higher success rates, and similar behavior shifts appear on external tasks. These are softer signals than pass rate, but they help explain what the policy is learning.40:T4e1,For a skim-level read, focus on the motivation and the construction pipeline. The introduction is worth reading because it clearly explains why issue/commit/test-driven environment generation is limiting. In the method section, pay attention to four pieces: task design and repository adaptation, execution-grounded test construction, execution consistency checks, and post-rollout filtering. The real contribution is not just “generate tasks from code,” but “close the loop with execution evidence and rollout-based quality control.”

In the experiments, prioritize three things: external benchmark transfer, the 1k/3k/5.5k scaling study against the uncleaned 8k baseline, and the behavioral analysis of repository exploration and self-checking. If time is limited, Figure 1, the dataset statistics, the scale/quality ablations, and the behavior plots should be enough to capture the main ideas in about 30–45 minutes.

Our analysis shows that entropy correction can preserve the e","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-18T15:16:51Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2609_21888.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这项工作解决的是预训练数据检测中的一个关键混淆：低 loss 可能来自训练集中见过，也可能只是因为文本本身容易预测。只用 likelihood 或平均 cross-entropy 做成员检测时，会把一些高度可预测的非成员文本误判为预训练数据。","importance":"它对预训练数据治理、benchmark contamination 检测、隐私和版权审计都有直接意义。方法贡献不在于训练新模型，而在于改进灰盒成员检测信号：把预测 loss 和预测熵放到同一个判别空间中，用熵校正区分“记住了所以低 loss”和“分布内且可预测所以低 loss”。对基座模型研究来说，这个角度提醒我们，预训练暴露信号不能只看 token likelihood，还要看模型在预测时的整体不确定性。","technical_approach":"作者从 loss-entropy 平面的几何解释出发。传统 likelihood-only 检测等价于在预测 loss 轴上放一个水平阈值；当非成员文本也很容易预测时，这个边界会失效。作者引入倾斜边界，用预测熵校正预测 loss，形式上接近 Helmholtz free energy：把 token loss 视为能量项，把预测分布熵视为不确定性项，用类似 L - βH 的分数度量每个 token 的残余 free-energy contribution，再聚合成序列级检测分数。理论分析给出两个要点：当成员和非成员的平均预测熵相同时，熵校正可以保留期望 membership gap 及其下界；当 loss gap 和 entropy gap 正相关时，合适的熵校正系数可以降低 gap 的方差，从而提高标准化分离度。作者还扩展到平均熵差不为零的情形。与已有方法相比，ETD 不只是对 likelihood 做参考模型校准或文本变换校准，而是直接利用目标模型 token-level 预测分布中的不确定性。","experiments":"实验报告显示，ETD 在多种设置下优于已有检测方法。相对基线，平均 AUROC 最高提升 3.5%，在 5% FPR 下的平均 TPR 最高提升 5.1%。这些指标选择比较合适：AUROC 衡量整体排序质量，TPR@5%FPR 更接近审计场景，因为误报率需要被控制在较低水平。实验结果与理论解释一致：当低 loss 非成员较多时，单独 likelihood 的水平边界容易把它们吸进成员区域，而熵校正后的倾斜边界能把这部分样本分开。文中还强调了鲁棒性，说明该分数在不同数据、模型或检测设置中不只是依赖某个特定阈值。","reading_guide":"建议精读 Figure 1 和方法推导部分。Figure 1 的 loss-entropy 几何图是理解整篇工作的入口：成员样本随训练暴露增加沿对角方向移动，而一部分非成员只是在 loss 维度低。理论部分重点看期望 gap 保持、方差降低条件、以及非零平均熵差的扩展；这些内容决定了熵校正什么时候会帮忙，什么时候可能引入偏差。方法实现部分关注 β 或温度系数如何选择、token-level 分数如何聚合、是否需要参考模型。实验部分优先看 AUROC 和 TPR@5%FPR 的分组结果，以及在可预测非成员上的错误类型分析。预计精读需要 45 到 60 分钟。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a core ambiguity in pretraining data detection: high likelihood can mean that a text was seen during pretraining, but it can also mean that the text is simply easy to predict. W=BA 的优化不稳定问题。LoRA、低秩预训练、压缩线性层、注意力中的 query-key 乘积都包含这类结构。问题不在于低秩表示本身，而在于分解不唯一：对任意可逆矩阵 M，(M^{-1}A, BM) 给出同一个有效权重 W。标准 AdamW 直接在 A、B 的欧氏坐标上更新，会把这个冗余自由度也当成真实优化方向，导致因子尺度漂移、学习率受限，甚至出现有效权重稳定但某个因子发散的现象。","importance":"对预训练研究的启发主要在优化器和参数化层面。它不是一个完整的 LLM 预训练 recipe，但指出了一个常被忽略的训练动态问题：低秩化、LoRA 化、注意力投影分解化之后，优化器看到的参数空间和模型实际函数空间并不一致。这个差异会影响可用学习率、稳定性和权重尺度。对于大模型训练中的低秩预训练、参数高效继续训练、Q/K 结构改造，这类几何约束优化器有实际参考价值。贡献量级更像是一个结构性 optimizer patch，而不是通用替代 AdamW 的训练范式。","technical_approach":"方法叫 Stiefel-AdamW。作者没有采用完整 fixed-rank quotient manifold 的做法，因为完整 GL(r) 不变性会和 AdamW 的逐坐标二阶矩预条件发生冲突。完整商流形在几何上更干净，但会迫使自适应优化器满足很强的等变性要求，难以保留 AdamW 最有用的 diagonal preconditioning。论文选择了一个折中：把一个因子约束到 Stiefel manifold，使其列或行保持正交，另一个因子仍在欧氏空间中优化。这样，原来的 GL(r) gauge symmetry 被收缩到 O(r) 正交对称性。好处是纤维变成紧集，因子不能沿着任意尺度方向发散；同时实现上仍接近 AdamW。具体更新流程是：欧氏因子按标准 AdamW 更新；Stiefel 因子先在 ambient space 中维护一阶、二阶矩，形成 AdamW 风格的预条件方向，然后投影到 Stiefel tangent space，最后用 retraction 拉回流形。retraction 可以用 Cayley transform、QR、polar decomposition 或 Newton-Schulz。论文强调 retraction 是模块化部件，选择会影响效率和少量最终性能，但不是主要变量。","experiments":"给出的全文内容主要覆盖动机和算法设计，未包含完整主实验表格。可确认的实验信息包括：作者在附录 B.1 和 Table 3 比较了不同 Stiefel retraction，结论是最终性能差异较小，最优选择随模型和任务略有变化；方法实现开销被描述为接近 AdamW，并具有低额外内存开销。论文声称在激进学习率和不平衡初始化下能提升稳定性，这和 LoRA 初始化中一个因子接近零、另一个因子承担主要尺度的情形直接相关。由于当前内容没有给出具体模型规模、任务、loss 曲线或精确提升数字，实验强度需要读者回到主实验章节核对。","reading_guide":"泛读建议用 20 分钟。重点看 Introduction 中对 gauge symmetry 的推导，这是论文最有价值的部分；再看算法章节中 AdamW moment accumulation、tangent projection、retraction 的顺序，确认它和 Riemannian Adam 类方法的差别。实验部分建议优先看学习率稳定性、LoRA 或低秩层实验、Table 3 的 retraction 消融。预训练研究员可以重点思考两个问题：第一，低秩预训练中是否应该把因子尺度作为 optimizer 设计对象；第二，注意力 Q/K 的乘积分解是否也会受类似 gauge 漂移影响。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies an optimization pathology in linear factorization blocks of the form W=BA. These blocks appear in LoRA adapters, low-rank pretrained or compressed layers, and the query-key product inside attention. The effective matrix W is invariant to the transformation (A, B) -> (M^{-1}A, BM) for any invertible M. Standard AdamW optimizes the two factors in Euclidean coordinates and ignores this gauge freedom. As a result, the optimizer can spend updates on non-functional directions, suffer from scale drift between factors, and become unstable under larger learning rates.","importance":"The main value for pretraining is in optimizer design for structured parameterizations. The paper does not propose a full LLM pretraining recipe, but it highlights a real mismatch between parameter space and function space when low-rank factorization is introduced. This matters for low-rank pretraining, LoRA-style continual training, model compression, and possibly attention projection designs. The contribution is best viewed as a targeted optimizer modification for multiplicative linear blocks, rather than a general replacement for AdamW across all parameters.","technical_approach":"Stiefel-AdamW constrains one factor of W=BA to a Stiefel manifold and leaves the other factor in Euclidean space. The authors deliberately avoid the full fixed-rank quotient formulation under GL(r), because full invariance is hard to reconcile with AdamW-style coordinate-wise diagonal preconditioning. By reducing the symmetry from GL(r) to O(r), the method removes the non-compact scaling directions that cause factor blow-up, while preserving most of AdamW's practical machinery. The Euclidean factor receives a standard AdamW update. For the Stiefel-constrained factor, first and second moments are accumulated in the ambient space, the preconditioned direction is projected onto the tangent space, and the parameter is retracted back to the manifold. The retraction can be implemented with Cayley, QR, polar decomposition, or Newton-Schulz iterations.","experiments":"The supplied full text mainly covers the motivation and method. It reports that different Stiefel retractions are compared in Appendix B.1 and Table 3, with only marginal impact on final performance and some model- or task-dependent variation. The method is described as having minimal implementation overhead over AdamW and low memory overhead. The claimed empirical benefit is improved numerical stability, especially under aggressive learning rates and unbalanced initializations such as those common in LoRA. The provided content does not include the full experimental tables, model sizes, task list, or exact improvement numbers, so those should be checked in the main experimental section.","reading_guide":"A focused skim should take around 20 minutes. Read the introduction carefully, especially the derivation of the gauge symmetry and the factor blow-up example. Then inspect the algorithm section to verify the order of moment accumulation, tangent projection, and retraction. In the experiments, prioritize learning-rate robustness, LoRA or low-rank layer results, and the retraction ablation in Table 3. For pretraining work, the useful question is whether low-rank parameterizations should be paired with geometry-aware optimizers rather than treated as ordinary Euclidean parameters.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"只看实验里的 LoRA/低秩 ablation；若开销低且稳定区间变宽，值得放进内部 optimizer 对照。","en":"Read only the LoRA/low-rank ablations; if overhead is low and the stable LR range widens, it deserves an internal optimizer comparison."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Emanuele Zangrando","Marco Sutti","Francesco Tudisco"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["optimizer","LoRA","low-rank","training-stability","Stiefel-manifold"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21039","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.21039","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}}]},{"id":"scaling","emoji":"📈","name":"Scaling 与计算分配","description":"Scaling Law / compute-optimality（Chinchilla/Kaplan/µP）、数据与模型规模扩展、test-time/inference-time scaling、agent/RL scaling、scaling 系数与可预测性方法论","papers":[{"id":"2609.21032","title":"Scaling Discovery through Test-Time Communication","abstract":"Science advances not in isolation but through collaboration, yet existing agentic systems capture little of this. next-token 风格接口，但预测对象从最终文本 token 变成了 edit history 中的 action token。这给 masked LM、diffusion LM、any-order LM 之外的非单调生成提供了一个较轻量的设计点。贡献量级偏探索性：形式化清楚，计算账算得比较直接，但实验还集中在 continuation，且当前只做 insert+move+Stop，没有真正的 delete/replace 破坏性编辑，也没有证明在大规模预训练下能替代常规 AR。","technical_approach":"核心设计是一个可变 canvas 加一个 cursor。模型每一步只预测一个动作：Insert(token)、Move(Δ) 或 Stop。Insert 在当前 cursor 位置写入 token，Move 让 cursor 前后移动，Stop 结束生成。模型不是直接看 canvas token，而是只看已经执行过的 action history；canvas 由这些动作确定性执行得到，只在合法性约束中使用。这样做避免了两类常见开销：一是不需要每步对所有插入槽位打分，二是不需要像扩散或迭代 refinement 那样反复跑整段序列。对于 prompt continuation，论文使用 prefix-seeding：先用确定性的 insert-only 动作把 prefix 写入 canvas 和 history，再从 prefix 末尾开始 continuation，并用 validity mask 禁止修改 prefix 区域。训练上使用 obfuscation-restoration supervision，把目标文本转成可学习的恢复轨迹，让模型学习下一步编辑动作。","experiments":"实验围绕 continuation 展开。评价包括 GPT-2 Large 作为外部语言模型的 evalPPL、arena pairwise preference、轨迹统计和 FLOPs 分析。论文报告 Reviser 在 arena 评价中明显优于 SEDD 和 MDLM 这类 diffusion / masked diffusion 生成基线；轨迹统计显示模型确实频繁执行 backward move 和 mid-canvas insertion，而不是退化成左到右生成。计算分析按统一 FLOPs 约定比较，Reviser 的主要额外成本来自 action steps/token 和 move 动作比例，而不是多轮全序列 denoising 或 slot scoring。文中还给出 300M Reviser 的定性轨迹例子，例如回到句中插入“at the time of publishing”来补充限定信息。摘要和给定正文没有提供完整数值表，因此具体 win rate、evalPPL 数字和各模型规模需要回到实验表格确认。","reading_guide":"泛读建议用 20–30 分钟。优先看 Section 5–6 的 canvas、cursor action、validity mask 和 executor 定义，这是方法是否简洁的关键。然后看
